1 
2330 台積電(TSMC)

報告日期： 2026/10/16 研 究 員：羅 金 鳳 (Sunny Lo ) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要：  
⚫ 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率受 N2良率調整問題而未有
顯著改善，不過因 N2 的出貨量快速大幅成長，有利未來整體
毛利率的改善。  
⚫ 積極擴充產能， N3將擴充三個新廠，且 N2的產能在 2027年
將擴充一倍，而後段的 Interposer 則與 Global  Foundry 進行
合作。  
⚫ AI五年的 CAGR >55%，不含 Agentic AI 所驅動的 CPU強勁
需求，顯見 AI的需求遠優於預期。  
⚫ 我們將 2026/2027 年的稅後 EPS由 106.63/142.76 元，上修
至 111.36/152.74 元。

一. 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率仍受 N2影響  
3Q26 AI因 AI持續帶動先進製程需求強勁， N3營收比重由 2Q26
的 30%上升至 33%，N2營收比重也由 2Q26 的 3%上升至 8%，
帶動台積電 3Q26 營收達 14,942 億元， QoQ +17.62 %，
YoY +50.95 %，在成本改善、高產能利用率 及製程的升級帶動下，
抵銷 2nm量產初期對毛利率的負影響下， 3Q26毛利率 66.41%，
達公司展望的上緣， 稅後 EPS 2 9.43元，優於預期。 4Q26在 AI
帶動的先進製程及先進封裝需求持續強勁下，預估 以美元對新台
幣 1:31.8 為基礎計算，預估 營收中 值約 16,949 億元，
QoQ+13.4 3%，YoY+ 62.02 %，符合市場預期，惟 仍受 N2量產良
率調整問題的影響，預估 4Q26 整體毛利率維持在 66%左右，，
單季稅後 EPS 32.81元，續創新高 。

二. AI需求強勁，將擴大投資及合作  
由於客戶及客戶的客戶持續釋出 強勁的需求，AI 需求 5 年(2025
～2030)的 CAGR將超越 55%，且此一數字不含 Agentic AI 所帶
動的 CPU需求，主因 CPU主要的應用除了 AI之外，亦應用於傳
統的 PC及非 AI領域，顯見 AI需求的延續性拉長且需求優於預
期，台積電將積極擴產滿足客戶需求，雖然 2026年資本支出維持
600～640億美元，但因持續的海內外擴產加上 2030 年在成本合
理及正面效益下， 會開始導入 Hi-NA EUV，因此，預估未來數年
的資本支出將大幅走高。  
投資評等：買進

     20 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：2440元 
     15.97 X 2027EPS

2

AI帶動 GPU、ASIC需求持續成長，但進入 Agentic AI 則是帶動
CPU需求強勁成長，驅動半導體榮景延續。由於目前需求遠遠大
過台積電所能供應的產能，且持續朝先進製程應用，因此，台積電
目前 N2已在新竹、高雄量產，且 2027年產能將會擴增一倍，應
用於智慧型手機、 HPC、AI，且 A14 將會在 2027 年進行風險性
試產，2028年量產； N3方面也會擴增三個新廠，台南廠的進度符
合預期， 1H27可量產，亞利桑那州第二個 Fab(N3)在 3Q27也可
開始量產 (比預期提前 )，熊本廠於 2028年裝機量產。 由於台積電
美國大客戶及美國政府 希望台積電在美擴大產能，因此，台積電將
會在亞利桑那州以外的德州擴產，會由台積電自已營運 Fab廠，
不過目前尚未有正式結論。 雖然看似擴充相當大的產能，但台積電
都已由上而上的評估且有紀律的擴產，在電力等關鍵指標方面也
都已考慮進去了。

除了先進製程需求強勁外，先進封裝的需求也相對緊俏，因此，台
積電在後段將與 Global Foundry 合作，由 Global Foundry 進行
Interposer 的生產，以滿足客戶需求 ，台積電則是衝刺利潤率更好
的先進製程。

三. 上修 2026/2027年稅後 EPS至 111.36 /152.74元 
2026年受惠 AI及 Agentic AI 需求超乎  預期的帶動下，台積電將
2026年全年的營收由 YoY+40%上修至 44%(以美元計價 )，因此，
我們預估台積電 2026 年營收 55,936 億元，YoY+46.85%，毛利
率 66.55%，稅後淨利 28,877 億元，YoY+68.1%，稅後 EPS由
106.63 元上修至 111.36元。2027年在 GPU、ASIC、CPU、XPU
需求持續強勁的帶動下， 台積電營運仍會是強勁成長的一年，我們
預估台積電 2027 年營收 77,218 億元， YoY+37.17%，毛利率
67.53%，稅後 EPS由 142.76 元上修至 152.74 元。

四. 投資建議維持買進，目標價 3055元 
在 AI需求強勁的帶動下， 先進製程產能持續緊俏，台積電除了積
極擴擴 N3產能外， 2027 年 N2的產能將增加一倍 ，在高階產品
應用上將取得高市佔率， 且在毛利率表現方面，因 先進製程領導地
位的穩固吸引更多訂單， 訂價策略的改變， 及良率的改善，帶動毛
利率維持高檔，帶動獲利 持續大幅成長， 因此，投資建議維持買
進，目標價 3055元(20X2027年稅後 EPS)。

貳、公 司 營 運 概 況

3 
一.公司簡介  
台積電(2330)成立於 1987年，為專業的 Fabless 半導體廠商，為
客戶生產的晶片廣泛地被運用在各種終端市場，例如行動裝置、高
效能運算、車用電子與物聯網等。 2019年，台積電及其子公司所
擁有及管理 約當年產能超過 1,200 萬片十二吋晶圓。在台灣設有
三座十二吋超大晶圓廠、四座八吋晶圓廠和一座六吋晶圓廠，並擁
有一家 100%持有之海外子公司 —台積電（南京）有限公司之十二
吋晶圓廠及二家 100%持有之海外子公司 —WaferTech 美國子公
司、台積電 (中國)有限公司之八吋晶圓廠。 台積電於 2011年第四
季開始導入 28nm，且開始加大先進製程技術的投資，於 2H12以
平面電晶體結構為基礎，在 20nm導入加強版的 HKMG 等技術，
並於 2014年進一步量產 16nm FinFET( 鰭式場效電晶體 )，持續推
進摩爾定律，儘管摩爾定律在 3nm以下將面臨物理極限，台積電
透過先進封裝製程延長摩爾定律，為高端產品 /客戶提供最先進的
技術。為因應美國製造優先及半導在在地化製造的需求，台積電於
2021年開始在美國亞利桑那州建廠，預計 興建六座先進製程廠、
2座先進封裝廠、 1個 R&D中心， 4nm已於 4Q24開始生產，預
估 3nm 也將在 2026 年進行量產，並且因為 AI 需求強勁的帶動
下，將加速 2nm及 16A的進廠速度，預估有機會於 2027～2028
年開始陸續量產。

二.營運概況及展望  
1、發展矽光子技術解決資料傳輸速度不足及訊耗損失問題  
10/2022 ChatGPT 推出後，其廣泛的推理能力，與使用者在各領
域以文字內容的方式對答如流，帶給使用者前所未有的體驗與 驚
豔，使用者呈現爆炸 性的成長，推升生成式 AI快速發展，使得 AI 
Server 呈現供不應求的現象 ，其不論在外觀的設計架構抑或 內部
的零組件規格甚至資料傳輸的頻寬及速度均較一般傳輸的 Server
大幅提升，且內部各元件的規格須同步提升才能使 AI Server 的強
大效能發揮最大的效用。

過往 Server 內部資料的傳輸採用銅線進行傳輸， 由於 AI Server
對資料傳輸的頻寬及傳輸速度的需求大幅提升， 銅線傳輸將產生
較大的訊號減損，使資料傳輸的品質不佳， 而以光做為資料傳輸的
解決方案，可解決資料傳輸訊號減損的問題， 因此，銅退光進的解
決方案勢在必行 。

矽光子的技術為銅退光進的最終解決方案， 但礙於技術及良率尚
不成熟，因此， CPO將為先行發展的技術，以 作為逐步達成光通
訊元件與主晶片直接在矽晶圓製程的階段即同時完成製作 的過渡
技術。在矽光子製作的過程中將以光波導取代 傳統銅線的傳輸，由

於光會沿著折射率較大的 物質前進，空氣的折射率為 1、水的折射
率為 1.33、玻璃的折射率為 1.46、二氧化矽的折射率為 1.6、矽
的折射率為 3.5，因此，光波導將選擇從矽晶圓上製作， 使光能進
行最佳的全反射，讓光能在光波導中的傳 送達最大的效率。由於矽
光子的發展為半導體未來的趨勢，大多數晶圓廠已投入研發，台積
電在此一趨勢下亦不缺席， 進行 40nm～90nm製程的矽光子技術
開發，預估 2025 年將利用 65nm 製程完成緊湊型通用光子引擎
(COUPE)的開發，支援可插拔式的連接器， 2026 年整合 CoWoS
封裝，將 CPO 與 Switch 晶片共同整合於同一基板上，完成共同
封裝光學 (CPO)的開發，未來將進一步將 CPO 與 XPU 共同整合
於矽仲介板上 ，實現晶片間更快的傳輸速度及較低的延時性。

圖一、矽光子發展示 意圖

資料來源： imec；宏遠投顧整理

2、透過 2.5D/3D 封裝延續摩爾定律  
矽為半導體重要材料，矽 1.0為平面式電晶 (Planar FET) 體時代，
由於電子遷移速度受到限制，製程節點 /閘極長度走到大約 28nm；
在 28nm以下進入到矽 2.0，為了改善電子元件漏電的問題， Intel
自 22nm 開始將平面世電晶體改成鰭式場效電晶體 (FinFET)，台
積電亦自 16nm開始將製程改為 FinFET，但當製程節點不斷往下
微縮至 5nm、3nm時，FinFET 將面臨摩爾定律的物理極限，即進
入矽 3.0時代，利用 2.5D及 3D封裝的技術可延續摩爾定律，台
積電在 2.5D封裝方面推出 CoWoS，Intel 推出 EMIB。CoWoS 在
晶片及基板之間加入 Si Interposer( 不具導電功能 )做為連接橋樑；
而 Intel 的 EMIB 則利用矽橋 (Silicon Bridge ；具有元件連通距離
短及反應速度快的特性 )做為多晶片之間連接的橋樑。

圖二、2.5D封裝示意圖

資料來源： Intel & 宏遠投顧彙整

在 3D封裝方面，台積電推出 3D Fabric 平台，Intel 推出 Foveros 
3D封裝架構。台積電 3D Fabric，主要為前段製程採用 Wafer on 
Wafer 及 Chip on Wafer 的 SoIC(系統整合單晶片 )方式，將晶片
進行 3D堆疊，然後結合後段先進封裝製程，包括 CoWoS及InFO，
將邏輯晶片及記憶體晶片進行 2.5D及 3D的封裝製程，達到縮小
IC 面積及提高效能的效果。而 Intel 的 Foveros 將不同製程節點
的晶片進行 3D封裝，包括 10nm製程的運算晶片、 22nm製程的
系統 I/O 晶片和 PoP(Package -on-Package)封裝的 DRAM 記憶
體。

圖三、3D封裝示意圖

資料來源： 網路 & 宏遠投顧彙整

公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

1 
2408 南亞科(Nanya Technology)

報告日期： 2026/10/13 研 究 員：翁浩軒  (Davy Wong)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 毛利率優於預期且將維持 8 成以上 
 2027 年 DRAM 供需仍緊俏 
 新廠 2027 年第四季加入貢獻

一. 3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

二. 4Q26 整體產業仍將維持供不應求態勢  
受惠於 AI 需求 ， AI 運算（GPU 、 TPU 、 CPU 及 ASIC）成為 DRAM 
需求成長主力，帶動高容量、高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求，
範疇從 HBM 、LPDDR5 進一步擴展至網路、儲存與系統管理等領
域。在傳統應用市場方面，由於 DRAM 供給不足與 ASP 上漲，
部分傳統應用終端出現產品降規或延長生命週期的現象，低階市
場則有回收 DRAM 支撐部分需求。供給端方面，因新廠建置、設
備交期、製程導入均需要較長的前置時間，目前供給仍維持緊俏，
智慧型手機、個人電腦及一般消費性記憶體供給受限，製造商與
供應鏈端的庫存水位普遍偏低 ， 隨著供應商依 LTA 長約進行產品
組合與產能規劃，有利未來供需穩定度。

三. 新廠預估 4Q27 開始投產 
公司 5A 新廠預計 2027 年第一季裝機，2027 年第四季開始投
產 ， 預計 2028 年底前達到每月投片量 3 萬片 ， 全產能目標於 2030 
年底前達每月 5 萬片，未來產能擴充均以客戶長期合約的 AI 專
案為主要目標。另外公司亦佈局先進封測領域，規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠 ， 預估 2H28 設備進駐 。 公司 2026
年前三季累計資本支出為 276 億元 ， 2026 年資本支出以不超過 697
億元為上限，其中生產設備資本支出約占五成。

四.  2026 / 2027 年獲利創高，預估 EPS 77.1 元/ 134.3 元 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：670 元 
       5 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：527.0 元 
     3.92 X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/12 收盤

展望 2026 下半年至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大 ， 接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 ， 小型
語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記
憶體需求持續上揚。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅增加產能，預估
2027 年新增產能有限，多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新
增產能實際貢獻要到 2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科新廠預計 2028 年放
量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，全產能將達 5 萬片/月。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 670 元 
南亞科為全球前五大 DRAM 廠，受惠於 AI 帶動之供給端改善，記
憶體產品漲價快速，南亞科今明年獲利創高，2028 年全球雖然有
新產能開出，但隨著 AI 由模型訓練與推理延伸至更多客製化邊緣
AI 應用，帶動記憶體位元需求長期成長。考量到 AI 應用滲透率提
升帶動記憶體需求，記憶體已成戰略物資，客戶亦開始簽訂長約，
參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 670 元，建議買進

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
南亞科成立於 1 995 年，為 DRAM （動態隨機存取記憶體）之研
發、設計、製造與銷售商，為全球第四大 DRAM 廠，市佔率約 
3%，由美光授權製程技術，其十二吋晶圓廠位於泰山南林園區，
月產能 6.8 萬片 ， 其中 20nm 製程約 3.8 萬片 ， 30nm 製程約 3 萬
片，公司產品廣泛應用於手機、PC、消費性電子產品，主要競爭
對手包括 SK Hynix、三星電子、美光、華邦電等。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. AI 帶動記憶體價格雙位數上漲 
全球 CSP 大廠持續加強投資 AI server 及相關基礎建設，全球九大
CSP 廠之資本支出持續高速成長，2026 年資本支出將達 9220 億元
(YoY+98%)，2027 年資本支出成長至 13830 億美元(YoY+50%)。
TrendForce 預估 2026 年 HBM 需求量年增 70%。而隨著 AI 應用由
LLM 模型訓練延伸至推論，CSP 廠擴大通用型伺服器布建，也使
記憶體拉貨重心由 HBM 延伸至 RDIMM ， 進一步推升 DDR5 價格。
3Q26 在 AI 與資料中心建置需求持續下記憶體仍嚴重供不應求 ， 儘
管價格基期已高，但預估 DRAM 合約價持續上漲，TrendForce 預
估第三季整體 conventional DRAM 合約價上漲 13-18% ， 第四季持續
上漲 10%~15%。供給方面，DRAM 三大原廠今年新增產能有限，
三星及海力士均以既有廠區調整及製程轉換為主，預估晶圓產能
分別增加 5%及 8%，而美光產能持平，三大原廠新廠產能開出並

明顯貢獻預估在 2028 年 。 預估 2026 年 DRAM 價格逐季調升 ， 2027
年新增供給有限下，價格易漲難跌。

2. 2027 年記憶體需求維持強勁 
展望 2026 第四季至 2027 年，AI 需求維持強勁，AI 及一般雲端伺
服器 DRAM 需求持續擴大，接下來逐步擴散至非雲端市場 (如: 
Edge、客製化)，小型語言模型推廣 AI 至邊緣裝置 (PC、手機、汽
車、機器人等)，新增客製化 DRAM 需求，整體記憶體需求持續
上揚。需求面依各應用別而言，Server 方面，AI 及高效能運算持
續驅動 HBM 與 DDR5、LPDDR5 需求，高階 e-SSD、BMC、NIC
對 DDR4、LPDDR4 及 DDR5 需求顯著; PC 及手機方面，高階 PC
與手機對 DDR5 及 LPDDR5 需求維持穩定 ， 然中低階需求疲弱; 消
費型電子市場方面，AI 及一般伺服器對 HBM 、RDIMM 需求持續
擴張，排擠一般消費型記憶體供給。供給方面，2026 年產業微幅
增加產能，聚焦於高階雲端與高附加價值產品需求，DRAM 大廠
優先分配產能於 DDR5 及 HBM 產品 ， 預估 2027 年新增產能有限，
多種 DRAM 產品將持續缺貨，三大原廠新增產能實際貢獻要到
2028 年才會顯現 。 南亞科第二代 10 奈米級製程(1B) 16Gb DDR5 產
品交運量已達 10%，第三代 10 奈米級(1C)及第四代 10 奈米級(1D)
製程技術試產中。公司 DDR5 營收約占 10%，並可視需求彈性調
整增加，同時持續供應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 產品以緩解市場缺口。
公司亦積極與策略夥伴合作拓展 AI 邊緣運算驅動的記憶體市場，
目前處工程驗證階段，預計於 2026 年底推出新產品。南亞科 2025
年資本支出 134 億元，2026 年資本支出預計不超過 697 億元，其
中生產設備支出約占五成，新廠預計於 2027 年第一季開始裝機，
2027 年第四季開始生產，2028 年放量，初期每月投片量 3 萬片，
預估 2030 年全產能將達 5 萬片/月。南亞科 2025 年位元銷售量成
長 50 多%，預估 2026 年成長 17~19%，預估 2027 年成長中低個位
數。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

1、3Q26 毛利率優於預期，EPS 25.4 元 
南亞科 3Q26 營收 1336.49 億(QoQ+61.9% 、YoY+611.7%) ，其中位元
出貨量季減 4~6% ， 平均銷售單價季增超過 60%(主要因反應長期合
約重新簽訂之調整)，匯率影響有利 1~3%，3Q26 毛利率上升至
85.7%，主要因平均銷售單價上升，營業費用季增 28 億元，因當
期及未來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利 876.27 億(QoQ+74.58%、
YoY+5505.16%)，EPS 25.4 元。

2、2026/2027 年營收獲利創高，預估 EPS 77.1 / 134.3 元

2026 OCP Global Summit –
MGX Upgrades Take Shape;
Maia 300 on Display
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Howard Kao

Howard.Kao@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2989

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
At the rack level, Maia 300 is the key incremental story, while
Nvidia/AMD exhibits offered limited new system-level hardware.
Nvidia’s MGX component upgrades are the main supply-chain
watch points, although PTFE and next-gen interconnect and
cooling connector (ZQD) adoption remains uncertain.

Nvidia/AMD –  focus on ramps rather than new platforms. Most ODM displays
were similar to Computex Taipei (early June), so timing of ramp is key. For Nvidia,
VR200 racks have started a small-volume ramp, with the Nvidia Groq 3 LPX rack
following in late 2026/early 2027 and standalone Vera CPU racks in late 1Q27/early
2Q27, based on our checks. For AMD, Helios remained largely unchanged with
recent checks, with racks ramping in 1Q27, with pilot-build observations pointing to

Shennan Circuits for EAM PCBs and Fositek/Parker for quick disconnects.
Maia 300 -  a larger in-house inference opportunity. Microsoft plans production
deployments in 2027 as it scales Maia toward multi-gigawatt deployments. Each
rack integrates 96 accelerators within 167 kW. Based on that, each gigawatt of
deployment would imply 5 to 5.5K racks, and we believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could
be the ODM partners, while our pilot-build observations point to ISU Petasys for the

accelerator PCB, AVC for fans, and Fositek/Staubli for quick disconnects.
PTFE - technical upside, but no confirmed timing of adoption. Nvidia is evaluating
PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon following the Kyber delay and showcased its
NVLink PTFE PCB with seven suppliers (though we think there are suppliers missing
from this list). Better signal performance and reduced reliance on Q glass are
potential benefits, but processing complexity, higher costs and yield/qualification

risks remain hurdles to commercial adoption.
Ultra Dense Interconnect - next-generation interconnect opportunity for FIT.
Nvidia showcased six suppliers for this next-generation interconnect, which is
intended to replace Amphenol’s current HD2 connector in midplane and cable
cartridge applications. However, we believe adoption is still about two years away. In
the meantime, the supply chain is working to improve yields and bring ASPs below
US$100, with pogo-pin supply currently a bottleneck. Our checks identify FIT as the

key design partner for Kyber’s planned board-to-board connector, a position that
bodes well for its supply share once the product ramps.
ZQD - higher flow density and an expected ASP premium. Nvidia’s new ZQD
targets higher flow rates per unit area and was displayed with 14 suppliers.
Although the supply chain is working to improve yields and lower costs, greater
design complexity should keep ASPs above current QD pricing of US$20–30.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 19, 2026 01:16 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Major Takeaways from OCP Summit 2026
ODM/OEM: Almost all the ODM/OEMs had a booth at OCP, but during our visit, we feel
most of the products were largely similar to what was showcased at Computex, such as
the Vera Rubin NVL72 rack, the LPX tray, the Vera standalone tray. So net, we think from a
hardware perspective, there was nothing new on Nvidia or AMD. We believe the Nvidia
VR200 rack is already starting ramp in small volume, with the LPX rack starting to ramp

later this year or early next year, and the Vera standalone CPU rack ramping late 1Q27 or
early 2Q27.
AMD: Wiwynn, Meta, and AMD each showcased the Helios rack, which was unchanged
from last year’s OCP display with one exception: this year’s version featured the actual
compute tray in place of last year’s dummy tray, allowing a closer look. That said, the
compute tray itself was not new to us, as we had already seen it at OCP Taiwan in August.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Shennan Circuits is one

of the pilot build PCB suppliers for the MI455 Helios EAM PCB.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek (MQD02) and
Parker (UQD04) are one of the pilot build quick disconnect connector suppliers for
the MI455 Helios compute tray.
• HPE displayed the Helios switch tray at the OCP Summit. We believe the other
supplier of the Helios switch trays is Celestica.
Microsoft: Maia 300 represents a major expansion of Microsoft’s in-house inference

infrastructure. Announced on October 12, 2026, with production deployments planned for
2027, it forms part of Microsoft’s commitment to scale the Maia family to multi-gigawatt
deployments and make it the primary inference platform for its products and services. The
design targets long-context reasoning, conversational AI, and multi-step agentic
workloads, with an emphasis on improving inference economics through coordinated
silicon, software, and system design.

Maia 300 rack integrates 96 accelerators within a 167 kW power envelope, providing
approximately 27.6 TB of aggregate HBM capacity. There are 4 accelerators per tray,
implying 24 compute trays per rack, including six high-radix switch ASICs that are
integrated into three liquid-cooled switch trays, providing the rack-local connectivity
required by all 96 accelerators. Microsoft projects 40% more tokens per megawatt than
leading GPU-based systems and up to 7× Maia 200’s rack-level token throughput, though

this takes into account that the Maia 300 rack  includes twice as many accelerators per
rack.
• The Official Microsoft Blog A two-tier Ethernet scale-up network connects up to
128 racks, or 12,288 accelerators, while liquid cooling removes more than 80% of
rack heat. Each compute tray includes 8x fans  (Exhibit 3 ), and based on our
observations at the OCP Summit, we believe AVC is one of the pilot build fan
suppliers for the sample compute tray.

• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe ISU Petasys is one of
the pilot build PCB suppliers for Microsoft’s Maia 300 AI accelerator.
• Based on our observations at the OCP Summit, we believe Fositek and Staubli are
pilot build MQD03 (quick disconnect connector) suppliers for the compute tray.

Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 3
• We believe Hon Hai and Wiwynn could be ODM partners for these racks.
• With each rack being 167 kW of power, one gigawatt of deployment would imply 5-
5.5K racks, assuming a PUE of 1.1-1.2.

Source: Microsoft.
Nvidia MGX Ecosystem
PTFE: We previously wrote that our supply chain checks suggest that Nvidia had initially
planned to adopt PTFE CCL for the very high-layer-count midplane in its Kyber
architecture. With Kyber now delayed, and Rubin Ultra no longer expected to use that
architecture, as previously discussed, the focus has shifted back to the Oberon
architecture. Nvidia is again evaluating PTFE for Rubin Ultra on Oberon, and at this year’s

OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE PCB, with a total of 7 suppliers, including
VGT, TTM, Taiflex, SYTECH, Shennan Circuits, Kinwong, and Flexium (Exhibit 4  & Exhibit
5 ). However, our checks indicate that actual adoption for Rubin Ultra remains uncertain.
However, we find the supplier list puzzling. Our checks suggest that WUS PCB and
Unimicron are also working on this solution, and that Flexium, which has collaborated
with other PCB suppliers, may handle only a few process steps. Our checks also indicate

that Shennan Circuits, WUS PCB and VGT are now the leading PCB makers for this
solution, with relatively higher yields.
Advantages of PTFE
• Best signal performance: the lowest Dk and Df of the mainstream options, below
even standard M9-class CCLs. This matters most where insertion-loss budgets are
tightest, i.e. the highest speeds and highest layer counts.
• Stable electricals: the low Dk/Df hold up across frequency and temperature, and
PTFE absorbs very little moisture, so performance is stable in use.

• Less dependence on Q glass: because PTFE’s intrinsic dielectric constant is already
very low, reaching the target Dk requires less (and in some hybrid constructions,
none) of the expensive, supply-constrained Q glass. That is both a cost lever and a
supply-security benefit.
Disadvantages of PTFE

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
• Mechanically soft: low modulus makes it difficult to handle and to hold to
tolerance.
• Difficult lamination: PTFE does not flow like a thermoset, so bonding needs either
very high temperatures or lower-melting fluoropolymer (such as PFA
(polyfluoroalkoxy))/thermoset bond layers; its high, non-linear CTE stresses
copper adhesion and complicates layer-to-layer registration, and pressing typically
requires vacuum or an inert atmosphere with longer cycles.

• Difficult drilling: the soft, gummy resin tends to smear and can tear, and any
ceramic filler is abrasive on tooling. Also, the hole wall will not accept electroless
copper without an added activation step (sodium etch or plasma) on top of normal
desmear.
• Higher cost and qualification risk: materials and fabrication are both more
expensive, the qualified supply base is narrower, and yields at very high layer
counts carry risk.

Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.

6
Exhibit 5: Nvidia PTFE PCB Supplier List
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
Ultra Dense Interconnect: Currently, Nvidia's NVL72 racks are using Amphenol's Paladin
HD2 connectors for VR200 NVL72 midplanes and cable cartridges. Our previous checks
done around Computex 2026 in June, indicate ASP could reach US$70-90+ per set. For
the next-generation Kyber racks, our checks have previously indicated that a new midplane
connector called "Voronoi" will be introduced, and FIT is the key design partner for this

new board-to-board connector. At this year’s OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased a new
connector it calls the “Ultra Dense Interconnect”, with six suppliers in total. This was our
first in-person look at the design. We believe adoption is still two years out, and the
supply chain is currently focused on improving yields and optimizing costs to bring the
ASP below US$100. Our checks suggest that pogo pin supply is currently a bottleneck for
this connector.

### Page 7

Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Exhibit 6: Nvidia next-generation Ultra Dense Interconnect
Source: Nvidia, Morgan Stanley Research.
ZQD: At COMPUTEX in Taipei earlier this year, we noted that our checks indicated Nvidia
was planning to introduce a new quick disconnect (QD) design for next-generation server
racks to achieve higher flow rates per unit area as cooling requirements rise. At this year’s
OCP Summit, Nvidia showcased that design, called ZQD, with 14 suppliers in total. Our

checks indicate the supply chain is also working to improve yields and optimize costs to
bring ZQD ASPs down. However, given the greater complexity of the design, we expect
ZQD ASPs to remain above current QD pricing of US$20–30 per set.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

CHPT
Valuation
We value CHPT using a PE-based approach that factors in earnings momentum and the structural shift towards high-end
testing. Our target price of NT$4,350 is derived from applying 35x to 2027E EPS, implying the company's average forward PE
since 2024 +1 SD, which we believe is justified given the company's entrenched position in the 2nm/3nm testing segment,
rising contribution from US AI GPU & AI ASIC projects, and the unique competitive advantage of its "All-in-House" vertical

integration model. With these structural drivers in place, and as we believe the market is starting to value it as an AI/HPC play
(rather than a consumer electronics stock), we expect the multiple to hold at a premium relative to its historical averages.
Risks
Our quant system rates the CHPT stock as High Risk for its share price volatility.
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price, and to our earnings forecasts, include: 1) margin

compression from lower-than-expected utilization rates; 2) execution delays in concentrated US AI GPU and ASIC programs;
3) intensifying competition in testing boards from global peers; and 4) structural gross margin dilution from a shift towards
high-end burn-in boards.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

萬海 (2615 TT)  Buy

近洋線進入需求旺季，給予投資建議為 Buy。  
投資建議 ：考量 4Q26 上旬美國線運價相對有撐，近洋線進入需
求旺季，目前 PBR 評價處於近年區間中下緣，給予萬海投資建
議為 Buy ，目標價 140 元。  
近洋線 4Q26 進入需求旺季 ：受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，
颱風因素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船
舶艘數、船舶周轉率下滑等因素影響之下，支撐美國線運價持
續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾
聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加所致。美國線方面，
10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆春
節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影
響而緊縮通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬，
通行等候天數增加進而降低美東線船舶周轉率，通航條件改善
後，將進一步減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。近洋線方面， 4Q26
進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。   
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元： 1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市
場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營收 336.41 億元，
毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營 業 利 益QoQ-7.86% ，
另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後
EPS 2.73 元。 2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收
QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認
列金融資產評價利益之下 ， 單季稅後獲利 115.35 億元 ， 稅後EPS 
4.11 元。 3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求
旺季，適逢氣候異常導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航
等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈
現上漲趨勢所致 。 預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億元 ， 稅後EPS 8.19
元。 4Q26 亞洲線進入需求旺季，價量有望齊揚；遠洋線進入需
求淡季，美國線運價走勢將較 3Q26 修正，預估單季稅後獲利
134.88 億元，稅後 EPS 4.81 元。整體而言， 2026 年供過於求壓
力較 2025 年舒緩之下，預估萬海 2026 年稅後獲利 556.72 億
元， YoY+76.94% ，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  公司 ESG 政策：  
(一 )企業對於因應氣候變遷或溫室氣體管理之策略  
1.萬海秉持對環境保護的承諾，除符合 IMO 國際公約規範之下，進行船隊
減排，減少空汙，並且落實  設備保養及改善，確保機器良好運作，以達達
到環保之要求，愛護地球，永續經營。  2. 萬海積極參與國際環保平台，透
過實際行動來推行節能減碳的環保策略：  (1)2015 年參與 Clean Cargo 
Working Group (CCWG) 組織，加強與同業分享環保知識、資訊、技  術的交
流，以推動節能減碳為目的。自 2017 年起依據 CCWG 新規範，萬海提供
CCWG 的船隊相關航  行、油耗數據須由驗證單位檢驗，萬海船隊已連續五
年 (2016 ~ 2021 年度 )排放數據均通過第三方  驗證單位 DNV 之驗證，此將
可主動吸引同為其會員的貨主、承攬業選擇使用符合其環保政策之航商。
(2) 持續參與世界航運協會 (World Shipping Council,WSC) ，針對各項航運事
務及環境保護議題，  進行討論。萬海為該協會會員，副董事長為該協會之
理事會成員。  (3)2019 年加入零排放聯盟 (Getting to ZeroCoalition) ，萬海
持續推動並實現國際航運減少碳  排放為宗旨，致力達成該聯盟目標。  
(二 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量目標  
依照 IMO 規定，以 2008 年為基準降低碳排率，  以 2021 年為止減少 30%
碳排放率，並預計於 2030 年可  以達到 50% 減排率目標，優於 IMO 要求；
未來將持續進行船隊汰舊換新計畫，並配以新型節能主機及  各式最新節能
設備，以達節能減碳、環境保護目標。  
(三 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量之預算與計畫  
1.預算：保護地球本為每一個企業之責任，本公司將以提升船舶性能，降低
船隊油耗，使用低硫燃  油，以降低溫室氣體排放，視該成本為必要性支出。
2. 船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan
(SEEMP) ：  依國際海事組織 (IMO) 之船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy 
Efficiency ManagementPlan, SEEMP) 規範訂定有效的能源效率管理制度，並
根據能源效率設計指標 (EnergyEfficient Design Index, EEDI) 作為新船能源
效率的標準，並據實揭露能源效率營運指標 (Energy Efficient Operational
Index, EEOI) 。  3. 萬海另依 IMO 法規要求制定 SEEMP PART II ，加入「燃
油消耗數據收集計畫」 (DataCollection Plan, DCP) ，藉由執行燃油消耗數據
的監測，管理和報告船舶於海上航行時所產生的二氧化碳排  放，並依法規
製作年度排放報告，經第三方查核認證後提交給國際海事組織。  
4Q26 近洋線進入需求旺季：  
3Q26 以來遠洋線走勢呈現分化。受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，颱風因
素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船舶艘數、船舶周轉率
下滑等因素疊加影響之下，支撐美國線運價持續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對
疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加
所致。  
美國線方面， 10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆
春節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影響而緊縮
通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬至 33 艘 (正常為 36 艘 )，

3 群益投顧 
通行等候天數增加進而降低美東線船舶周轉率，通航條件改善後，將進一步
減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。  
近洋線方面， 4Q26 進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。  
圖一、 SCFI 指數

資料來源：群益投顧彙整  
燃油價格處於高檔震盪：  
油價方面，近期新加坡低硫油價格持續回落。美伊戰爭前約 500 美元 /噸，
戰爭後一度上漲至 1,120 美元 /噸，近期則來到近 890 美元 /噸。以目前運價
水準而言，已完全轉嫁燃油價格變動對營運成本造成的影響。  
新船下水規劃：  
公司至 2025 年底船隊達 116 艘，運力共計 57.27 萬 TEU。 2026 年預計交付
6 艘船舶，包括 2 艘 7,000TEU 及 4 艘 8,700TEU 船舶， 2027 年預計交付 1
艘 8,700TEU 及 10 艘 16,000TEU 船舶。  
營收比重方面， 2025 年美國線比重約 23% 、中東印度線比重約 27% 、亞洲
近洋線比重約 37% 、南美線比重約 13% 。  
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元 : 
1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營
收 336.41 億元，毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營業利益 QoQ-
7.86% ，另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後 EPS
2.73 元。  
2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收 QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營
業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認列金融資產評價利益之下，單季稅後獲利
115.35 億元，稅後 EPS 4.11 元。  
3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求旺季，適逢氣候異常
導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運
價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈現上漲趨勢所致。預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億
元，稅後 EPS 8.19 元。

4Q26 亞洲線進入需求旺季，價量有望齊揚；遠洋線進入需求淡季，美國線
運價走勢將較 3Q26 修正，預估單季稅後獲利 134.88 億元，稅後 EPS 4.81
元。

後獲利 556.72 億元， YoY+76.94% ，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

考量 4Q26 上旬美國線運價相對有撐，近洋線進入需求旺季，目前 PBR 評
價處於近年區間中下緣，給予萬海投資建議為 Buy ，目標價 140 元。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

RA 황현식 
hyeonsig.hwang@meritz.co.kr Long Trading 구간  
3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후: ESS ↑, BEV ↓  
LG에너지솔루션의 3Q26P 잠정실적 발표 이후 배터리 섹터는 11/3 미국 중간선거
를 전후로 Trading Long 투자의견이 유효하다. 미국 중간선거 이후 특정정당 우세와 
별개로, 미국 Ex-China ESS 공급망 프리미엄은 유효하다. 동사 역시 그간의 ESS 
사업 난제(단위당 수율, 팩 병목)는 점진적 해소될 점이다. 반면 시장의 BEV 반등 기
대감에 동의하지 않는다. EU 프랑스와 독일의 내연기관 퇴출시점 지연 움직임 및 미
국 연비규제 정책 폐지 결정으로 전통 완성차 고객사들향 BEV 배터리 출하 부진은 
여전하겠다. 미국의 BEV 정책 변화시점은 28.11월 대선으로 지연될 전망이다.

ESS 모멘텀: 한국 제3차 ESS 중앙계약시장 사업자 선정 모멘텀 
대다수 시장 참여자들은 2026년 연말까지 LG에너지솔루션의 미국 ESS 60Gwh  규
모의 잔여 수주 달성 가시성에 집중한다. 해당 ESS 사업 수주 가이던스 관건은 시기
보다 방향성이다. 단, 당사가 주목하는 연말 모멘텀은 한국 정부향 제3차 ESS 중앙
계약시장 사업자 선정으로 판단한다. 3차 규모는 1.13Gw로 1&2차 계약 규모(합산 
1.13Gw) 대비 확대되었다. 또한 언론보도상 LG에너지솔루션은 해당 수주를 위한 핵
심 4대 소재 등 전 Value-chain 국산화를 선언한 바 있다. 해당 수주 확정시, 동사
를 비롯한 해당 Value-chain에 긍정적 모멘텀이 예상된다.

투자의견 Buy, 적정주가 51만원 제시   
26년 연중 지정학 Risk, 에너지 대전환 영향 등에 전력 인프라 수요는 꾸준할 전망
이다. 향후 국내 이차전지 산업은 ‘L’ Shaped Cycle 전망, 반면 동사는 스마트카 T사
향 원통형 전지 출하량 증가, ESS 제조업 이상으로 변모하는 과정으로 산업 평균 성
장률 Outperform 가능하다. 투자의견 Buy 유지, 적정주가는 추정치 상향에 51만원
을 제시한다.

Meritz Research  2026. 10. 15

Document 249: Anthropic招股书“刺激了贪婪”！CoreWeave领衔AI交易反弹

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic招股书“刺激了贪婪”！CoreWeave领衔AI交易反弹.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 招股书披露上半年营收 163 亿美元、同⽐暴增近 14 倍，打消了市场对 AI 烧钱不可
持续的担忧。 CoreWeave 单⽇涨 8% 领衔 AI 基础设施板块全线反弹。 Anthropic 对算⼒的⻓
期资本承诺，直接验证了新云服务商、存储及光学连接等基础设施赛道的需求前景。
摘要：
Anthropic 周⼀提交 IPO 申请，招股书显⽰其今年上半年营收达 163 亿美元，同⽐增⻓近 14 倍。这
⼀数字远超部分投资者此前的悲观预期，令华尔街情绪迅速逆转。
周⼆，纳斯达克综合指数收涨 1.2% ，延续周⼀ 0.8% 的涨幅。 AI 云计算、存储、光学连接等基础
设施板块全线⾛⾼，此前因利率担忧和宏观压⼒遭受重挫的个股领涨。
CoreWeave 周⼆⼤涨 8% ， Nebius Group 涨幅更达 11.2% ，成为当⽇最亮眼的两只 AI 基础设施
股。
存储板块同步受益。西部数据涨 7% ，希捷科技涨 3.7% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

此外，戴尔周⼆收涨 7.5% ，今年以来该股已因 AI 服务器热潮⼤幅⾛⾼。光学连接股 Coherent 涨
6.4% ， Lumentum 涨 5.9% 。 AI 连接芯⽚供应商 Credo 和 Astera Labs 分别⼤涨 10.8% 和 10.5% 。
Anthropic 对算⼒的⻓期资本承诺，直接验证了新云服务商、存储及光学连接等基础设施赛道的
需求前景。值得注意的是，这波反弹发⽣在埃隆 · ⻢斯克周末公开唱空的背景下 —— ⻢斯克预⾔
新⼀代云服务商将⾯临资本枯竭。他称， 200 多家新型云服务商将⾯临整合，有些公司能够挺过
去，但数量不会太多。市场显然选择⽤脚投票，⽆视了这⼀警告。
招股书数据 “ 令⼈震惊 ” ， IPO 或严重超额认购
InvestorPlace 分析师 Louis Navellier 在给客⼾的报告中写道， Anthropic 的 S-1 招股书 " 正在引发
⼤量兴奋情绪 " 。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

他直接点出了核⼼数据：上半年营收 163 亿美元，同⽐增⻓近 14 倍。
尽管公司仍处于运营亏损状态， Navellier 认为这种 " 令⼈震惊 " 的营收增速将推动 IPO" 严重超额认
购 "—— 即市场对股票的需求将远超实际发⾏量。
⻓期资本承诺，是基础设施股上涨的真正逻辑
Tortoise Capital ⾼级组合经理 Rob Thummel 向 MarketWatch 解释了这波⾏情背后的投资逻辑。
"Anthropic 对资本⽀出做出了⻓期的重⼤承诺， " 他说。随着算⼒需求持续攀升，⽀撑这些部署
的基础设施需求也将同步增⻓ —— 这直接利好向客⼾出租算⼒的新云服务商。
Thummel 还特别提到 Anthropic 为锁定未来算⼒⽽签订的⼀系列合同，称这反映出市场对 AI 基础

设施需求 " 将在相当⻓时间内持续强劲 " 的信⼼。
Lazard ：市场对这类资产仍有充裕胃⼝
Lazard Asset Management 组合经理 Celine Woo 向 MarketWatch 表⽰，尽管 Anthropic IPO 的定
价和需求尚不明朗，但这份招股书本⾝⽆疑是⼀个看涨信号。
" 从投资者⻆度来看，市场对持有这类商业模式的资产仍有充裕的胃⼝， " 她说。
Anthropic 的商业模式 —— 以及其竞争对⼿的模式 —— 都需要⼤量芯⽚和算⼒来维持运转。这⼀
逻辑正在为整条 AI 供应链提供⽀撑。
芯⽚巨头财报助攻，⼤科技公司烧钱不⽌
Morningstar ⾸席欧洲股票策略师 Michael Field 在周⼀的报告中指出， ASML （涨 2.9% ）和台积
电（涨 3% ）均因先进芯⽚技术需求旺盛⽽超预期，并对⾏业前景给出了 " 乐观展望 " 。
但⼤科技公司的 AI ⽀出同样没有放缓迹象。 Meta 上周发布强劲财报后，周⼀宣布计划通过股权
融资筹集最⾼ 950 亿美元。 Alphabet 、微软和亚⻢逊则将于本周晚些时候相继公布财报。

Document 308: Expected 2027 TMT supply points to a new record

Source PDF: all report pdf/Expected 2027 TMT supply points to a new record.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

European TMT gross bond supply may reach €150bn in 2027,
driven by tech funding for AI and data centres rather than
traditional telecoms. Strong cash ﬂows and credit quality
support issuance, but disciplined funding will be key to market
absorption
Jan Frederik Slijkerman
AI and data centre investment is expected to be the main driver of TMT bond
issuance in 2027
We predict another year of strong supply for the European TMT
sector. We expect €150bn of gross issuance and €103bn of net

issuance, given €47bn of debt redemptions. The additional
issuance is not coming from the traditional telecom or media
issuers, but from global tech companies.
As we expect the data centre build-out to continue in 2027, issuers
are likely to return to the European investment-grade credit market
to raise part of the required funding. Meanwhile, we do not see a
major reason for traditional sectors to reduce or increase

Source: Dealogic, ING
Telecoms supply
We will break down the main factors aﬀecting debt issuance,
starting with FY27 capex requirements. While we expect capex as a
percentage of sales to decline, aggregate capex is likely to increase

slightly. Companies such as Orange and Vodafone are integrating
recent acquisitions and may need to invest additional capital to
realise merger synergies. We therefore do not expect the lower
capex-to-sales ratio to translate into less issuance. Nevertheless,
BT and KPN are exceptions, as both clearly intend to reduce capex
in FY27.
Low-single-digit EBITDA growth gives companies some capacity to
issue additional debt while maintaining stable leverage. However,

higher interest rates provide an incentive to keep debt levels in
check. We therefore expect the increase in debt issuance to be
driven mainly by M&A. Several debt-funded acquisitions of smaller
networks could generate around €5bn of net issuance. A further
€3bn could come from US operators raising euro-denominated
funding for spectrum payments in 2027.

### Page 3

Technology supply
In 2027, we believe the AI build-out will continue. Tech companies
currently report healthy returns on investment, demand for AI
services continues to outpace supply, and companies want to
maintain their market positions. We therefore expect further capex
growth in 2027, with the large hyperscalers investing close to
US$1tr in ﬁscal 2027.
As in 2026, we expect these companies to ﬁnance their
investments largely from operating cash ﬂow, but also through

debt issuance, with additional funding coming from European
markets. We expect hyperscaler data centre operators to issue at
least €60bn in 2027, somewhat above the approximately €35bn
issued year-to-date in 2026, including issuance by NTT.
In addition to the €60bn of issuance from hyperscaler data centre
operators, we expect €35bn of net supply from other technology
issuers. Companies are likely to pursue acquisitions and raise
additional funding for investment, including semiconductor,

software and payment services companies. Including €8bn from
telecom issuers, this brings total TMT net supply to €103bn. This
would translate into gross supply of approximately €150bn, which,
in our view, is close to the amount the European market may be
able to absorb.
Supply discipline will be important
Companies will need to issue prudently and provide clear guidance
on future funding plans, as spreads may only tighten once the
market no longer expects substantial new supply. Nevertheless,

current spreads look attractive relative to the issuers’ high ratings,
with much of the widening driven by supply and liquidity eﬀects.
Relatively wide CDS spreads also suggest that technical factors,
rather than a material deterioration in credit risk, are weighing on
valuations. This combination of high credit quality and attractive
spreads should attract new money to the investment-grade
market.

### Page 4

Challenge 1: Does debt issuance reduce
credit quality?
First, we expect the hyperscaler data centre operators Amazon,
Microsoft, Alphabet, Meta and Oracle to grow their combined
EBITDA by US$230bn, or 29%, in FY27, to around US$1tr. Moreover,
with the exception of Oracle, these companies are expected to
operate with limited net debt, with Microsoft holding more cash
than debt. The main caveat is their signiﬁcant lease commitments,
many of which are not yet reﬂected in balance-sheet lease

rating agencies will attach greater weight to these liabilities,
particularly if returns on investment start to decline. In our view, this
is not a material concern for 2027 and may never become one,
given the sector’s strong growth and ability to fund a signiﬁcant
part of investment from operating cash ﬂow. Companies also retain
ﬂexibility to scale back future investment that has not yet been
committed if returns start to disappoint.
Challenge 2: Do credit benchmarks

accommodate more supply?
Do European credit markets have the capacity to absorb additional
supply from technology companies? We think the current share of
technology issuers remains modest. Alphabet accounts for only
1.16% of the iBoxx € Non-Financials index, while Amazon and IBM
account for 0.68% and 0.91%, respectively.
In the US, Oracle’s share of more than 2% in the iBoxx $ Domestic
Non-Financials index illustrates that individual technology issuers

can reach materially higher benchmark weights, while several high-

inclusion of eligible new bonds in benchmarks also creates a strong

error.

Document 338: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?
Date:    6 hours ago
Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Irrational Exuberance In S&P
500 FEMO?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Chris Geirman / Unsplash
Are analysts' earnings expectations for the S&P 500 irrationally
exuberant? So far this year, FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum, as
measured by actual earnings per share) has been soaring, while FOMO
(fear of missing out, as reflected in P/E multiples) has been mostly
retreating. Much of that earnings optimism is concentrated in the S&P 500
Information Technology sector, particularly the Semiconductors industry,

### Page 3

where the AI investment boom has fueled extraordinary demand, pricing
power, and profit margins. But can these conditions persist? Let's take a
closer look:
(1) FEMO overwhelming FOMO. The S&P 500 is up 13.3% ytd, despite a
13.5% contraction in its forward P/E. That's because FEMO has
overwhelmed waning FOMO, with forward earnings jumping 31.0% since
the start of the year (chart).
The divergence is even more striking in Information Technology. The

sector's forward earnings has soared 65.5% ytd, while its forward P/E has
plunged 25.9%. The S&P 500 IT stock price index has risen 22.6% ytd,
powered by FEMO (chart).

### Page 4

Industry analysts remain remarkably bullish on the S&P 500's
fundamentals. Forward revenues is up 15.7% y/y, while forward earnings
is up 39.0% (chart).
(2) Semiconductors driving FEMO. Much of that optimism originates in
the IT sector, where forward revenues and earnings are up 57.2% and
91.4% y/y, respectively (chart).
Digging deeper reveals that the sector's extraordinary FEMO originates
largely in the Semiconductors industry, where forward revenues and

earnings are up 121.7% and 176.0% y/y, respectively (chart).

### Page 5

Indeed, semiconductors now account for a record 55.0% of the IT sector's
forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
The growing importance of semiconductors extends to the broader
market. Semiconductors now account for a record 20.4% of the S&P 500’s
forward earnings and 15.8% of its market capitalization (chart).

### Page 6

That makes the industry's earnings trajectory increasingly important for
the entire index. Analysts expect Semiconductor revenues and earnings to
grow 61.3% and 74.0% over the next year, respectively. More remarkably,
they expect earnings growth to average 66.1% annually over the next five
years.
These extraordinary expectations help explain why analysts project IT
sector earnings growth of 44.8% over the next year and 42.0% annually
over the next five years (chart).

In turn, analysts expect S&P 500 earnings to grow 19.0% over the next
year and an average of 26.1% annually over the next five years, the latter
approaching a record high.

### Page 7

(3) Pricing power fueling Semiconductors' FEMO. To determine
whether S&P 500 FEMO shows irrational exuberance, we need to assess
whether Semiconductor FEMO does so. Much of the industry's
extraordinary earnings growth reflects unprecedented profit margin
expansion. The industry’s forward profit margin nearly doubled from the
2023 lows to a record 51.8% during the week of October 20 (chart).
The explanation is straightforward: Demand for chips has surged as

hyperscalers and neocloud providers have raced to build AI infrastructure,
while supply is capacity constrained. The resulting pricing pressures are
clearly evident in the PPI for electronic components & accessories (up
26.8% y/y through September) and the import price index for computer &
electronic product manufacturing (up 12.4% y/y through September)
(chart).

### Page 8

(4) Will Semiconductors' pricing power persist? That's the critical
question for assessing whether Semiconductor FEMO is irrationally
exuberant. Investors already seem skeptical. The Semiconductor
industry's forward P/E has plunged to just 14.2 despite surging earnings
expectations (chart). In other words, unlike the tech bubble of 1999, any
irrational exuberance in FEMO has been mostly offset by depressed
FOMO!
The goods news is that demand for chips should remain strong. The nine

largest cloud service providers are expected to spend $957 billion on
capex this year, up 106% y/y, and more than $1.5 trillion in 2027. But
supply is starting to catch up. Global semiconductor manufacturing
capacity is expected to expand 5% this year and another 6% in 2027,
potentially easing shortages and price pressures.
(5) Irrational earnings exuberance? We suspect there is some irrational
exuberance in Semiconductor FEMO, particularly in analysts' consensus

long-term earnings growth expectations. Today's demand-supply
imbalance supports extraordinary profits, but sustaining current pricing
power and margins over a longer time horizon seems optimistic as
capacity expands. With Semiconductors accounting for more than one-
fifth of S&P 500 forward earnings, that exuberance is reflected in the
broader index.
Nevertheless, FEMO remains robust beyond Semiconductors. Analysts
expect Q3 earnings growth of 30.7% y/y, with positive growth across all 11

sectors (chart).

### Page 9

Our diffusion indexes also show that an increasing share of S&P 500
companies is experiencing rising forward revenues and earnings (chart).
Combined with expectations that semiconductor demand will continue to
outpace supply, at least over the near term, this suggests that FEMO
remains fundamentally rational.
💡

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this

Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

Document 366: October ECB cheat sheet_ French malaise shouldn’t prompt a dovish turn

Source PDF: all report pdf/October ECB cheat sheet_ French malaise shouldn’t prompt a dovish turn.pdf
Report source: ING Think

### Page 1

Markets will scan the European Central Bank announcement on
29 October for any signs that French bond turmoil has
triggered a dovish rethink. We don’t think it has, and expect
the ECB to leave the door open for a December hike, which
should shield rates and the euro from a major adjustment. As
usual, we also look at three alternative scenarios to our
baseline

Francesco Pesole, Michiel Tukker and Carsten Brzeski
The ECB is expected to leave rates on hold this week at 2.5%
This is our market preview of October's European Central
Bank meeting; you can ﬁnd our macro preview here.
In September, the ECB eﬀectively brushed aside rising bond market
concerns with a hawkish rate hike. This week, a hold is widely
expected. The key question is whether the latest turmoil in French

### Page 2

and other European bonds is enough to trigger a dovish shift in the
ECB’s message.
We don’t think we are there yet. But equally, we admit that a more
dovish tone on further tightening would probably be the ﬁrst line of
defence against more bond volatility, before exploring any bond-
buying measure.
Four scenarios for October
As usual, we look at four scenarios for this meeting. As in previous
instances, the market reaction will depend as much on the

statement and press conference as on the now-customary tip to
the media on Thursday afternoon. In September, the message was:
“ECB oﬃcials expect more tightening, with October in play”.
A hike this week would, however, be a big surprise (close to zero
chance priced in), as inﬂation has remained well-behaved, France
has prompted a dovish repricing and President Christine Lagarde
has sounded a bit less hawkish.
In a meeting without new projections, the wording around

December will be crucial. We expect the door to remain open for a
December move, with a broadly neutral and balanced message to
markets. Signalling that a hike by year-end is “likely” would be a
slightly hawkish surprise in our view, even if markets are pricing in
20bp.

### Page 3

Rates look for predictability amid fragile
sentiment
Rates have started to deviate from their tight correlation with oil,
but global factors remain an important driver. Risk sentiment is
more fragile, and not just because of the French turmoil, but also
because of renewed AI jitters. So whilst markets will listen carefully
to Lagarde, we don’t expect this week’s meeting to trigger a major
repricing. A stronger dedication to a December hike would nudge a

hawkish repricing, but with more than 20bp already priced in, the
reaction should be contained. Both the ECB and markets
understand the outlook is shrouded in uncertainty, so any forward
guidance will be weighed against that backdrop.
A bigger surprise to markets would be a 25bp hike, but this would
not be taken lightly by investors, especially if December remains in
play too. Without clear second-round inﬂation risks, an unexpected
tightening of ﬁnancial conditions while sentiment is fragile could

trigger a broader risk-oﬀ move. Expect French government bond
spreads to widen and equities to continue their drift lower. We
might even see 10yr Bund yields fall on the decision, as growth
concerns return.
EUR could do with some ECB protection
The euro has become less sensitive to the latest moves in French
bond spreads, with undervaluation to short-term fair value still
around 1-1.5%. Still, the common currency has been hurt not only
by the build-up of a ﬁscal risk premium, but also by the repricing

lower in ECB rate expectations because of the French turmoil.
This week’s meeting is unlikely to have the same FX impact as
September’s, but currency investors will be looking for any cracks in
the ECB’s tightening bias as a sign that bond market concerns
could prompt a dovish rethink.
That probably leaves the balance of risks on the downside for the
euro. Still, our baseline is for no pushback against lingering hawkish
bets, leaving the euro broadly stable after Thursday.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
WESTPAC NOWCAST Q3
2026: SECOND ESTIMATE
Growth falters through the quarter
16 October 2026
Key points
• The second estimate from Westpac-Now
points to GDP growth of around +0.5%qtr
in Q3 2026 and 2.1%yr. This is slightly lower
than the first estimate of +0.6%qtr. This

still represents a slight step up in growth
from Q2 2026 but masks some loss of
momentum over the course of the quarter.
• Our Monthly Activity Index deteriorated
noticeably in August and September.
Weakness has become increasingly
broad-based, with labour and business
condition indicators among the
largest drags and relatively few areas
providing a meaningful offset.
• The economy is coming under increasing
pressure as the cumulative impact of
higher interest rates combines with

renewed disruptions in global energy
markets and a deepening housing
downturn. With another rate hike likely in
November, these pressures are expected
to build further heading into 2027.
• Overall, the economy is becoming
increasingly reliant on the upswing in
business investment to support growth.
This narrow base of growth has left the
balance of risks less favourable. Our central
case remains one of slowing rather than
stalling. Westpac-Now’s current model

dynamics point to growth moderating
to around +0.4%qtr in Q4 2026. Our
formal forecasts have growth slowing
to a softer +0.2%qtr pace (see here).
• Our final Westpac-Now estimate for Q3
2026 will be published on 20 November.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
In November 2025, we introduced Westpac-Now (see here),
a continuously-updated measure of current activity in the
Australian economy. Westpac-Now draws on more than
60 high frequency economic and financial variables, using
advanced statistical techniques to provide a timely pulse

check on economic momentum. It is the first Australian
model to incorporate internal banking data, enhancing its
predictive power.
This note provides the second estimate of GDP growth for the
September quarter 2026, using domestic and international
data released up to and including the September Labour Force
Survey (released 15 October 2026). Updates are published
monthly (see Appendix A for the release schedule).
Westpac Monthly Activity Index: activity falters as

headwinds mount
After showing tentative signs of improvement through the
second half of 2025, the Westpac Monthly Activity Index has
weakened markedly over 2026. The Index moved back into
negative territory in February and has declined further since.
While there were some signs of stabilisation around mid-year,
this proved to be short lived with the Index falling to –0.88 in
September, outside of the pandemic period, the weakest read
since 2013.
The economy is coming under pressure from multiple

fronts, with key headwinds coming from higher interest
rates, renewed disruptions to global energy markets and a
deepening in the housing downturn. Among households,
spending held up surprisingly well through the first half of the
year despite historically weak sentiment, but more recently
Growth momentum on shaky ground
spending outside of fuel has started to cool (see here). At
the same time, the labour market has continued to soften,
with unemployment reaching a cycle high of 4.7% (see here).

Meanwhile, the business sector is also showing signs of
softening, with sentiment deteriorating and conditions holding
at weak levels (see here). Business credit growth remains
a notable exception, although even here there are tentative
signs of easing (see here).
This deterioration is particularly evident in our heatmap.
Negative contributions have become both broader and more
pronounced through 2026, with the weakness intensifying
in August and September. Labour and business condition

indicators are now the largest sources of weakness, while
spending and confidence have also deteriorated materially.
Credit and capacity utilisation indicators have been
comparatively less negative, but there are currently few areas
providing a meaningful offset to the broader softening.
Our indicator heatmap decomposes changes in the Monthly
Activity Index into group-level contributions, allowing us to
assess which components are driving movements in activity.

For ease of interpretation, indicators are grouped into broad
categories (see the Appendix for further details). For example,
the labour market category includes both official ABS Labour
Force Survey outcomes and timelier survey-based measures of
labour demand and job advertisements.
Westpac-Now: Resilient growth outlook slowing not
stalling
Recall that Westpac-Now is a refined indicator that combines
our Monthly Activity Index with advanced econometric

techniques to estimate GDP growth. As previously noted,
the fit is not expected to be exact. Measured GDP includes
Neha Sharma
Economist

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
components that are not well captured by high-frequency
activity indicators, such as imputed rents for owner-occupiers,
housing-related ownership transfer costs, public and business
inventories, and depreciation.
To bridge monthly and quarterly data, we use a MIDAS

framework, allowing us to combine mixed-frequency inputs
and incorporate partial information for the current quarter
when producing GDP forecasts.
Based on current model estimates, Westpac-Now’s second
estimate for Q3 2026 points to GDP growth of 0.5%qtr
(with a 70% confidence interval of –0.1%qtr to 1.0%qtr)
and 2.1%yr, representing a slight downgrade from the first
estimate of +0.6%qtr but still a step up from the 0.4%qtr
(2.1%yr) outcome recorded in Q2 2026. While aggregate

growth appears to have held up through Q3, the underlying
picture became softer as the quarter progressed. At the
second decimal place the estimate is 0.46%qtr, leaving it
close to rounding down to 0.4%qtr.
With several headwinds now bearing on the economy
simultaneously, there is a growing risk that the slowdown in
activity becomes more pronounced than currently anticipated.
At the same time, the strength of the business investment
upswing provides an important counterweight that should

help cushion the broader slowdown.
Looking ahead, model dynamics suggest growth will tick
down to 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026 before picking up in Q1 2027.
However, these projections remain much more tentative,
given their reliance on projected inputs rather than realised
data. In addition, heightened uncertainty, particularly around
the renewed tensions in the Middle East conflict, means risks
around these forward estimates are larger than usual.
September Quarter data: a softer end to the quarter

Data released in the first month of the September quarter
indicated that the economy has picked up momentum relative
to the previous quarter, with our first nowcast estimating Q3
growth at 0.6%qtr, in line with the 0.6%qtr “pure forecast”
based solely on data available up to June 2026.
However, the flow of data since then shows downside risks to
growth have begun to materialise. Incorporating the August,
followed by the September (to date), data both yielded

nowcasts of 0.5%qtr (with a slight softening at the second
decimal place). This contrasts with Q2 2026, when weakness
emerged early in the quarter, in April, before subsequent data
pointed to stabilisation.
Note that monthly activity estimates are more uncertain
than normal when inflation is shifting. Faster price growth
can cause nominal indicators, such as consumer spending,
to rise more strongly even though ‘real’, inflation-adjusted
spending may be softening (and vice versa). The inclusion

of ‘real’ indicators, such as employment and banking
transaction volumes, helps mitigate some – but not all – of this
uncertainty.
Bottom line:
Westpac-Now points to GDP growth of around 0.5%qtr
(2.1%yr) in Q3 2026, suggesting aggregate growth held up
reasonably well through the quarter, although the flow of
data points to softer momentum as the quarter progressed.
The substantial upswing in business investment remains
an important source of support, helping to offset mounting

weakness elsewhere in the economy.
Looking ahead, the accumulation of headwinds is expected to
see growth slow further towards year-end, with Westpac-Now
currently pointing to growth of around 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026.
Downside risks around the outlook have increased, but for now
our central case remains one of slower growth rather than a
material downturn.

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

Subject: What Will Warsh Do?

What Will Warsh Do?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
What Will Warsh Do?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 20 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Olav Ahrens Røtne / Unsplash

I. The Fed: To Hike or Not To Hike?
The Federal Reserve announces its next interest-rate decision on
Wednesday, October 28. According to CME FedWatch, the federal funds
futures market assigns an 84% probability to a pause. The market expects
a 25bps hike in December and roughly 50bps of tightening over the next
six months (chart).

### Page 2

The two-year Treasury yield remains roughly 90bps above the federal
funds rate, consistent with expectations for further tightening (chart).
Why are markets pricing in such a low probability of an October hike
despite September's hot CPI and PPI reports? The explanation may lie
more in Fed commentary than in the economic data:
(1) Fed officials favor patience. New York Fed President John Williams
and Vice Chair Philip Jefferson, who alongside Chair Kevin Warsh form

the influential troika, have signaled little urgency to hike in October.
Governor Christopher Waller suggested that hikes need not occur at
consecutive meetings, while Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle Bowman
also favors patience. Together, their forward guidance has steered the
markets toward pricing in an October pause. Even Warsh's refusal to

### Page 3

provide guidance at the IMF-World Bank Annual Meetings in Bangkok on
October 16 was interpreted as supportive of a pause.
(2) Warsh's three problems. The markets remain heavily influenced by
the very forward guidance that Warsh wants to abandon. This creates
three challenges: First, other Fed officials have continued to provide
guidance despite Warsh’s preference to move away from it. Second, that
guidance influences bond yields, making it harder for Warsh to get a clean

signal from the bond market. Third, that risks boxing him into policy
decisions that he may oppose, as he could be reluctant to surprise the
markets.
(3) The solution. We suspect Warsh would prefer an October hike.
Following the FOMC's September 16 decision to raise the federal funds
rate by 25bps, Warsh said he would be “hard pressed to describe broad
financial conditions as restrictive,” characterizing the hike as removing a
dose of accommodation.

Raising rates in October, even if the markets aren't fully pricing in such a
move, could help Warsh address his three problems. It would signal that
forward guidance from other Fed officials, even influential members of the
traditional troika, may no longer carry the weight it once did. That could
encourage investors to place less weight on Fed commentary, giving
Warsh a clearer signal from the bond market about the appropriate path of
monetary policy. In turn, it would reduce the risk of getting boxed in by

market expectations shaped by forward guidance that Warsh does not
necessarily agree with.
(4) Our call. We estimate the neutral federal funds rate at 4.25%–4.50%
and believe the Fed should return to that range, reversing last year's three
25bps insurance cuts. Those cuts were intended to protect against labor-
market weakness (that we considered overblown) and were made at a
time when the Fed was too complacent about inflation.
We believe that Warsh should try to persuade his colleagues to hike next

week. Then again, he might not do so because the midterm elections are
too close.
II. US Economy

### Page 4

The latest US economic data suggest that monetary policy remains
accommodative, exerting little restraint on economic activity or inflation:
(1) Consumer spending. Redbook same-store sales rose 9.7% y/y
during the week of October 16. The four-week average climbed 9.1%, its
fastest pace since early July and well above the 2025 average of 5.8%
(chart).
(2) Labor market. The latest weekly ADP data show private-sector
employment rising by an average of 18,500 jobs per week over the four

weeks through October 3, implying roughly 74,000 jobs per month (chart).
This suggests that the labor market remains in good shape, supporting
continued brisk consumer spending.

### Page 5

(3) Import prices. September's data on import prices point to persistent
inflation pressures. The import price index surged 8.1% y/y, the fastest
pace since July 2022 (chart). The index excluding petroleum imports rose
6.0%.
Import prices from the newly industrialized Asian countries surged 12.5%
y/y in September, mostly reflecting booming AI-related demand for
semiconductors from South Korea and Taiwan (chart). That suggests the
AI buildout will remain inflationary for now, before AI brings the fruits of

disinflationary productivity growth.
(4) GDP. The Atlanta Fed's GDPNow model estimates Q3 real GDP
growth at 3.5% (saar). Real final sales to private domestic purchasers, a
key gauge of underlying demand, is projected to rise 4.7%, a pace last
matched in Q1-2023. Real consumer spending is expected to grow 3.8%,

### Page 6

clause.
